# Requirements traceability

Generated from `docs/requirements.yaml` by `tools/render_requirements.py`; edit the YAML, not this file.
Built Fri 2026-10-16 23:18-23:58 PDT by an 11-agent workflow (Claude Opus 5.5 on behalf of Logan Walker, GhostWalka), from every page of the slides and the brief, including the organizers'
corrections: G4 hangar 850 kg, not 900 kg; Three of the five possible sets of four exceed the 10 t margin.

**How to use it.** Each row says how we address a requirement, links the files and WORKLOG decisions
that do it, gives an honest status, and asks one brainstorming question for direction and scope.
Rows marked **Called out** are named explicitly by the slides or brief and must be answered at a
granular level (WORKLOG DEC-24). When work changes how a requirement is addressed, edit its row in
the YAML, re-render, and cite the ID in WORKLOG rows and entries (WORKLOG rules 8 and 9).
Status: done = finished and evidenced; drafted = a draft exists for review; planned = in the plan,
not built; gap = nothing addresses it yet; not-applicable = out of scope by the brief.

## Gaps

Rows with status gap. Called-out rows still only planned are counted in the summary above.

- **R-060** (called out): Must-have: a documented way to add a new guest card.
- **R-083** (called out): Should-have: the over-consuming guest is handled by throttle, warn or shed, with rationale.
- **R-088** (called out): With reduced crew (6) installation windows are shorter and rarer.
- **R-137**: The swapped file is chosen by the jury, so the governor must validate and handle an unseen priority file safely.
- **R-148** (called out): A scoring rationale names the verdict, the bound resource, its condition (loop temperature), the free amount and what the guest needs, with units.
- **R-171** (called out): Scenario A setting: ship alongside, stopped, on shore power, in 16 °C water.
- **R-173** (called out): Scenario A starts with three guests already installed, chosen by the team.
- **R-200** (called out): Scenario C crew level 6 corresponds to the monitoring mission.
- **R-201** (called out): Scenario C crew level 0 corresponds to night transit.
- **R-205** (called out): Demo baseline: 60 m vessel.
- **R-207**: The chart's event spikes sit near 00:17 and 00:22 on the axis while the callouts say 00:08 and 00:12; treat the callout times as authoritative.
- **R-237** (called out): Reference vessel length overall is 60 m (matches the 60 m vessel in the demo baseline).
- **R-238** (called out): Drawing carries a vertical 6 m dimension labelled LOB (ambiguous label, likely a depth or height figure); confirm against the ship profile in the brief before using it.
- **R-239** (called out): Propulsion is two pods (labelled twice); propulsion is a power consumer and affects course and noise.
- **R-270** (called out): Power reserve is a function of ship speed.
- **R-278** (called out): Alongside means moored, stopped and often on shore power.
- **R-323** (called out): Envelope covers slow and cruise speed bands; admission uses the lower of their reserves.
- **R-362** (called out): G1 loses 30 percent capability if own radar transmits nearby, unless 'quiet radio' is declared.
- **R-461** (called out): The glossary is the authoritative meaning of every domain term in the brief and Annexes; no maritime background is assumed.
- **R-483** (called out): Silence forbids machinery above minimum, manoeuvre and deck machinery such as winches and hangar doors.
- **R-500**: Start from a Kubernetes resource request and design for what it cannot express.
- **R-514**: Copernicus Marine provides sound-speed and layering; registration is required and activation can take hours, so register early.
- **R-522**: Start the synthetic data generators in hour 1.
- **R-539** (called out): Avoid learned behaviour an adversary could study and exploit.

## Problem framing and resource classes

| ID | Requirement and source | How we address it | Links and decisions | Status | Brainstorm question |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R-027 | **Called out.** Governor must answer whether there is enough power for a new payload. *(slides p3: "Is there enough power?")* | Data and arithmetic exist; the check does not. assumptions.yaml: guest reserve 200 kW cruise and 100 kW one genset down; peak +250 kW (+100 degraded) for at most 60 s; two 150 kW mission lines; our assumptions 0.9 genset load limit, 400 kW hotel, 55 kW auxiliaries, 150 kW hotel shed on trip. Tests prove the five cards' sustained draw is 126.5 kW (fits 200), G5+G4+G3 charging is 105 kW (over 100), and the budget closes (810-400-55 = 355 kW; 405-250-55 = 100 kW degraded). Planned ledger lines: power total, port line, starboard line. | [Power section](../config/assumptions.yaml), [Power tests](../tests/test_brief_arithmetic.py), [Ledger line classes](ARCHITECTURE.md#data-contracts), [Power assumptions](PHYSICAL_ACCOUNT.md#physical-account-draft), [DEC-15](../WORKLOG.md), [CON-08](../WORKLOG.md) | planned | When one 150 kW line saturates while the total does not, should yes-if offer a line swap through placement before it offers displacing a guest? |
| R-028 | **Called out.** Governor must answer whether there is enough cooling for a new payload. *(slides p3: "Cooling?")* | assumptions.yaml heat: 160 kW at 12 °C falling linearly to 120 kW at 30 °C, x0.6 degraded, 1 kW heat per kW electric, inertia +30 kW for 60 s once every 600 s, mast guests air-cooled at most 2 kW; admission judges cooling at 30 °C. Tests check 160, 120, 133.33 kW (24 °C, Scenario B) and 72 kW degraded at 30 °C, and that the four chilled-water brief cards (125 kW sustained) do not fit 72 kW. Planned ledger keeps heat as its own line next to power. | [Heat section](../config/assumptions.yaml), [Cooling tests](../tests/test_brief_arithmetic.py), [Constraint ledger](../GAMEPLAN.md#explainability-by-construction-the-constraint-ledger), [DEC-15](../WORKLOG.md) | planned | Should every admission print both the power and the heat margin side by side, even when power binds, so the jury always sees the coupling? |

Ceiling = tighter of degraded power (100 kW) and cooling at current seawater (160 kW at 12 C to 120 kW at 30 C, x0.6 degraded), minus 5 kW headroom (power.shed_headroom_kw). Steps come from each card's modes ladder (G5 45 -> 8 -> 0 kW; G2 20 -> 6 -> 1 -> 0; G4 35 -> 10 -> 2 -> 0), priced as mission weight x capability lost / kW freed; all throttles before any shed; mission-critical last; safety-critical never listed; ties by guest id; power and heat rechecked after every step; restore in reverse with a hold-off. Precomputed every tick as 'if a genset tripped now', so the first action is a lookup. A trip counts only on 2 of 3 signals. While generator state is uncertain, new admissions are judged as if one genset is down. If a mission suite's load is ever needed, it fits GAMEPLAN's 'host commitment' role (counted, never shed) next to hotel load and the 20 % compute reserve. | [GAMEPLAN: One ledger, one manifest language](../GAMEPLAN.md#one-ledger-one-manifest-language), [DEC-15](../WORKLOG.md) | not-applicable | Do we model the CMS as one more host commitment, or leave it out entirely? |

Rules and priorities are readable YAML/JSON.
